#### Variable handling (detection limits, units, roles)

* **Detection limits.** NCHS releases below-LLOD results already filled with LLOD/sqrt(2) and flags them with a comment-code variable (0 = at or above the detection limit, 1 = below the lower limit). The released value is kept unchanged in `value`; the matched comment code is in `lc_variable` / `lc_code`, and `below_lod` = (`lc_code` == 1). Comment codes are matched to analytes by the NHANES naming rule (`<P>X<core>` <-> `<P>D<core>LC`, truncated to 8 characters), then by label (comment-code label minus 'comment code' = analyte label minus units, or the analyte's parenthesised abbreviation, e.g. URDMEALC 'NMEA' -> URXNMEA 'N-Nitrosoethylmethylamine (NMEA)'), then by a unique name prefix; 722 comment codes matched, 0 unmatched. The serum neurofilament file flags the upper limit separately (SSNFLH, not ingested) and the lower limit in SSNFLL (used as the comment code).
* **Units.** When a file carries both conventional and SI versions of the same analyte (label identical up to the unit), only the first-listed (conventional) version is ingested (27 SI duplicates dropped); analytes published only in SI units (e.g. methylmalonic acid, serum folate forms) are kept.
* **Roles.** 1079 analyte variables; 38 urinary creatinine variables (kept, role `urine_creatinine`, for dilution adjustment); 14 specimen-process variables kept with role `process_variable` (urine collection volumes/times, the HDV re-testing flag); 36 questionnaire items bundled in the blood-VOC files (VTQ*) and 1 character variable not ingested; 84 sample-weight variables carried per row as `weight_variable` / `weight_value`.
* **Categorical results** (serology, STI, HPV, celiac, TB IGRA) keep the NCHS code in `value`, its label in `value_label`, and `result_positive` = 1 (Positive / Weakly positive / Reactive) or 0 (Negative); indeterminate / not evaluated codes stay NaN.
* **Subsamples.** Each file's weight variable identifies the sampled people: WTSA2YR / WTSB2YR / WTSC2YR (one-third environmental subsamples A/B/C), WTSAF2YR (morning fasting), WTSOG2YR (OGTT), WTFAS2YR (fatty acids), WTSVOC2Y / WTSVS2YR (blood VOCs), WTSH2YR (2013-2014 blood metals, a half sample), WTALD2YR / WTALDS2Y (aldehydes), WTANA2YR (autoantibodies), WTFSM (special smoking sample), WTSS* (surplus-specimen projects). A participant not sampled for a component has no rows for it; nothing is imputed here. Files without a weight are measured on the full MEC sample within the component's age/sex eligibility (e.g. HSV 14-49, HIV 18-59, oral HPV 14-69, klotho 40-79 surplus); those restrictions are in each codebook's Target field (saved `docs/*.htm`) and in `nhanes_lab_variables.csv`.
* **Special smoking sample** (`*S_G`/`*S_H` files, weight WTFSM): overlaps the main subsample-A files heavily (same participants measured once, released twice with different weights) and adds participants who are only in the special sample (column `special-only` above); rows are flagged `special_sample`.
* **Oral microbiome (2009-2012; only the 2011-2012 participants are in this project).** Alpha diversity (observed ASVs, Faith's phylogenetic diversity, Shannon-Wiener, inverse Simpson) at a rarefaction depth of 10,000 reads averaged over NCHS's 10 rarefactions, and genus relative abundance (DADA2, rarefied, SILVA v123 annotation) for the genera present in >= 5% of the 2011-2012 samples (zeros are kept as measured zeros). Participants aged 14-69 with a sequenced oral rinse.
* **Not individual-level / not ingested**: pooled-sample files (BFRPOL, PCBPOL, PSTPOL, DOXPOL, POOLTF), withdrawn files (SSEVD_G/H, PAHS_H), the fasting questionnaire, the urine pregnancy test and household tap-water fluoride (see the decision table).

## Source: nih_reporter

## DATA AUDIT — NIH RePORTER (Project API v2)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| source_id | nih_reporter |
| Source (dataset/API name, exact files/endpoints) | NIH RePORTER Project API v2, `POST https://api.reporter.nih.gov/v2/projects/search` (one request per page, limit 500, sorted by appl_id asc). Exact criteria JSON for all 76 queries in `queries.json`. |
| Publishing organization | National Institutes of Health, Office of Extramural Research (RePORT); records include non-NIH HHS/VA agencies that report to RePORTER |
| Retrieval date (UTC) | 2026-09-23T20:27:23+00:00 to 2026-09-23T20:36:10+00:00 (HTTP responses cached under data/_http_cache; reruns reuse them) |
| Source version / release | RePORTER API v2.0 (swagger `info.version`); no dataset release number. Latest `date_added` among retrieved records: 2026-09-19T17:04:02 |
| Source update date / cadence | RePORTER is refreshed weekly by NIH. FY2026 was still open at retrieval (fiscal year ends 2026-09-30), so FY2026 counts are incomplete. |
| License / access conditions | U.S. federal government data, public; no key. NIH asks for <= 1 request/second and large jobs off-hours; this module throttles to 1 request/second (105 requests in total). |
| Unit of observation | One fiscal-year award record (`appl_id`): an application funded in one fiscal year. A multi-year project (`core_project_num`) has one appl_id per fiscal year; multi-component awards add subproject records with their own appl_ids. |
| Sample size (actual, as ingested) | 9,342 unique appl_ids (3,020 core projects, 549 organizations, 838 subproject records) from 36 condition-term queries and 40 augmented queries, FY2015-FY2026 |
| Geography (resolution, vintage) | Awardee organization address (city/state/ZIP), U.S. only. ZIP5 -> 2024 Census ZCTA internal point (`zcta_centroid`) via `crosswalk.zip_to_geo`; if the ZIP has no ZCTA, RePORTER's own org coordinate verified to lie in the org's state (`point`). County = 2024 county containing the point. RePORTER's coordinate is always kept as `reporter_lat/lon`. |
| Person-level? | no (PI names are public award metadata about investigators, not study participants) |
| Geographic? | yes, facility/organization points (layer 4), not population burden |
| Omics? | no |
| Wearable? | no (grant text may mention wearables; no measurements) |
| True participant linkage across modalities? | no; not applicable (no participant data) |

### Files / endpoints retrieved
All files are in `data/raw/nih_reporter/`; hashes in `MANIFEST.json`.

| File | Bytes | sha256 | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `docs/RePORTER_Project_API_V2_data_elements.pdf` | 690,439 | `6d3a236bd4dce6df...` | https://api.reporter.nih.gov/documents/Data%20Elements%20for%20RePORTER%20Project%20API_V2.pdf |
| `docs/reporter_swagger_v2.json` | 34,158 | `22cbaf09b1640c27...` | https://api.reporter.nih.gov/swagger/v2/swagger.json |
| `queries.json` | 166,095 | `a6336b785b4d056e...` | https://api.reporter.nih.gov/v2/projects/search |
| `query_hits.csv` | 786,777 | `5b96b32d024e3eda...` | https://api.reporter.nih.gov/v2/projects/search |
| `records.jsonl.gz` | 29,816,809 | `5ccc5ce27fea2fde...` | https://api.reporter.nih.gov/v2/projects/search |

* `records.jsonl.gz`: every unique RePORTER record returned (full API JSON, one per appl_id).
* `query_hits.csv`: query_id x appl_id membership for every query.
* `queries.json`: for each query, the exact request body (`criteria` with `fiscal_years`, `advanced_text_search` {operator: "advanced", search_field: "projecttitle,abstracttext,terms", search_text}), API total, RePORTER `search_id` and web URL, and a log of every page request.

Query design:
* Condition queries: one per `search_terms` entry in `configs/conditions.yaml`; search text is the exact quoted phrase, for example `"postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome"`.
* Augmented queries (SPEC section D), for each demo-cluster condition (long_covid, me_cfs, pots, dysautonomia): `("term 1" OR "term 2" ...) AND (<measurement variants>)`. Measurement variants: wearable = wearable OR wearables; biomarker = biomarker OR biomarkers; diagnostic = diagnostic OR diagnostics; sensor = sensor OR sensors; imaging = imaging; autonomic = autonomic; microvascular = microvascular; capillary = capillary OR capillaries; digital health = digital health; point of care = point of care. Plural variants are there because the API does no stemming (measured: "wearable" 5,902 hits, "wearables" 2,728, the OR of both 5,964; "biomarker" 135,407, "biomarkers" 82,679, both 143,201; FY2015-2026, all projects).
* Fiscal-year splitting: needed only when a criteria set returns more than 15,000 records (offset cap 14,999). Split used in this run: False.
* Queries with zero API hits: cond:long_covid:post_covid_condition, cond:dysautonomia:autonomic_nervous_system_disorder, cond:eds_hsd:joint_hypermobility_syndrome, cond:ptlds:post_lyme_disease_syndrome, cond:ptlds:persistent_symptoms_after_lyme, cond:post_infectious_syndrome:post_acute_infection_syndrome, cond:post_infectious_syndrome:infection_associated_chronic_illness, cond:post_infectious_syndrome:post_infectious_fatigue, aug:me_cfs:digital_health, aug:me_cfs:point_of_care, aug:pots:wearable, aug:pots:capillary, aug:pots:digital_health, aug:pots:point_of_care, aug:dysautonomia:point_of_care.
* API total vs retrieved mismatches: none.

### Key variables
| Variable | Meaning | Units | Downstream use |
|---|---|---|---|
| appl_id | RePORTER application id (one fiscal-year award record) | id | primary key of nih_projects |
| project_num / core_project_num | full award number / award number without type, support year and suffix | id | groups fiscal years of one project; parent/subproject grouping uses (project_num, fiscal_year) |
| subproject_id, is_subproject | component of a multi-project award | id / bool | totals count the parent's award_amount instead of its subprojects' (measured consistency below) |
| fiscal_year | federal fiscal year of the award | year | per-FY counts |
| project_title, abstract_text, public_health_relevance, terms, pref_terms | text; `terms` is RePORTER's thesaurus-expanded concept list | text | measurement text mining; match_tier |
| pi_names, pi_profile_ids, contact_pi_name, contact_pi_profile_id, principal_investigators (JSON incl. is_contact_pi) | investigators | text / id | investigator counts per organization |
| org_name, org_ipf_code, org_city, org_state, org_zipcode, org_country, org_dept_type, org_type_name | awardee organization | text | research-site aggregation |
| award_amount | total-cost award obligation of this appl_id; equals the sum of `ic_fundings[].total_cost` in 8,847 of 8,847 records that have both | USD | funding totals (de-duplicated) |
| direct_cost_amt, indirect_cost_amt | direct and indirect cost fields as published; they do **not** sum to award_amount in 621 of 7,971 records that have all three | USD | context only; never summed in place of award_amount |
| agency_code, admin_ic_abbreviation, funding_ics, ic_fundings (JSON) | awarding agency, administering IC, funding ICs with per-IC costs | code | agency breakdown |
| activity_code, funding_mechanism, award_type | mechanism (R01, U54, SBIR...) | code | capability type |
| project_start_date/end_date, budget_start/end | project and budget periods | date | recency |
| is_active | RePORTER active flag at retrieval | bool | active projects |
| spending_categories(_desc) | NIH RCDC spending categories | id / names | agreement check against text search |
| zip5, lat, lon, county_fips, state_fips, geocode_method | ZIP5 -> ZCTA internal point -> 2024 county (`zcta_centroid`); RePORTER's state-verified point when the ZIP has no ZCTA (`point`) | degrees / FIPS (zero-padded strings) | facility layer geography |
| reporter_lat, reporter_lon | RePORTER's own organization coordinate, always kept | degrees | canonical point only for the 973 rows whose ZIP has no ZCTA; otherwise kept for comparison |
| matched_condition_ids, augmented_terms_hit | every condition / measurement term whose query returned the record, including terms-field-only and likely-false-positive links | text | convenience only; use `nih_project_conditions` or the helpers for the precision view |
| match_tier (links) | where the matched phrase occurs locally: title_abstract / terms_only / not_found_locally | category | precision filter |
| rcdc_category_assigned (links) | whether NIH's RCDC category for the condition is on the record | bool / null | precision check |

### Sample sizes

#### Projects per condition
`projects (appl_id)` counts fiscal-year award records; `core projects` counts distinct multi-year projects.
`award obligations USD (dedup)` counts each appl_id once and excludes subprojects whose parent award is also in the condition set. `naive sum USD` is the plain sum and shows how much double counting that removes.
These are **award obligations** (total cost, summed over fiscal years 2015-2026), not expenditure, and not the portion of a grant spent on the condition: a grant that mentions the condition anywhere contributes its whole award.

Why the precision view is the default, measured: among NIH-administered records of conditions that have an NIH RCDC spending category, the share carrying that category by match tier. Only fiscal years in which that category appears on at least one retrieved record are counted ({'long_covid': 'FY2022-FY2025', 'me_cfs': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'pots': 'FY2020-FY2025', 'fibromyalgia': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'lyme_disease': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'ptlds': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'migraine': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'endometriosis': 'FY2015-FY2025'}). RCDC categories are assigned after a fiscal year closes: 0 of 813 NIH FY2026 records have any category, and 0 of 552 non-NIH records do.

Terms-field-only matches carry the NIH category less often than title/abstract matches for: endometriosis, fibromyalgia, long_covid, lyme_disease, me_cfs, migraine, pots; not for: ptlds (ptlds is compared against the broader "Lyme Disease" category, so any Lyme grant passes). RCDC is NIH's own classification, not ground truth: a category is assigned when a project is judged relevant enough to report as spending, so even title/abstract matches do not all carry it.

* Unique appl_ids across all conditions (union): 9,342. Union award obligations (dedup) = $5,887,032,117 (naive sum $6,184,801,443; 607 subproject rows had their parent in the table).
* Parent vs subproject amounts, measured over the 179 (project_num, fiscal year) groups in the table that hold a parent and at least one subproject: parent = sum of the retrieved subprojects in 57; parent larger in 112 (its other subprojects did not match any query); parent **smaller** in 10 (1U54NS137199-01 FY2024, 5P50AR070600-03 FY2018, 5U54AI138370-03 FY2019, 5U54AI178855-07 FY2024, 5U54AI178855-08 FY2025, 5U54DK123755-04 FY2023, 5U54DK123755-05 FY2024, 5U54NS105541-04 FY2020, 5U54NS105541-05 FY2021, 9U54AI178855-06 FY2023), where the subprojects sum to $7,114,608 more than their parents in total. RePORTER documents the parent amount as including all subprojects, and the parent amount always equals the award's IC funding, so the parent amount is what the totals count. A condition matched only by subprojects of such an award therefore carries the subprojects' larger sum.
* 1,023 appl_ids matched more than one condition. The per-condition totals above therefore overlap: their sum ($6,543,018,946) is **not** a total and must not be reported as one. Use the union figure.
* Active: 1,183 (12.7%) appl_ids carry `is_active = true`.
* Grouping condition `post_infectious_syndrome`: all 3 of its search phrases returned 0 records.

Note: several phrases return near-identical totals (e.g. "myalgic encephalomyelitis" vs "chronic fatigue syndrome", "long haul COVID" vs "post COVID syndrome"). The `terms` field carries thesaurus synonyms, so a phrase matches through the concept even when it is absent from the title and abstract. `match_tier` makes this visible; `terms-field only` rows are concept-level matches and lower precision.

#### Measurement-augmented queries (unique appl_ids)
* `api_hits`: records RePORTER returned for `(condition terms) AND (measurement variants)`.
* `both_in_title_abstract`: the condition phrase and the measurement term both occur in the title, abstract or narrative, not only in the terms field.
* `local_regex_hits`: of the condition's own hits, how many contain a measurement variant anywhere (title, abstract, narrative or terms), by local regex. This is an independent check of the API's boolean logic.
* `api_only` / `local_only`: disagreements between the two.

A hit means a grant mentions the measurement term. It is not evidence that the measurement works for the condition.

### Geocoding
Method, in order:
1. U.S. organizations only. Foreign postal codes are never read as ZIPs; a Nigerian 900246 would otherwise land on ZIP 90024 in Los Angeles.
2. ZIP5 to the Census ZCTA internal point with `crosswalk.zip_to_geo`: `geocode_method = zip5_as_zcta_internal_point`, resolution `zcta_centroid`.
3. Only if the ZIP has no ZCTA (unique or PO-box ZIPs of large campuses): RePORTER's own organization coordinate, accepted only when it falls inside a 2024 county of the organization's stated state. `geocode_method = reporter_org_point_state_verified`, resolution `point`.

Results:
* 8,739 (93.5%) projects geocoded: 7,766 by ZCTA and 973 by the verified RePORTER point. That is 8,739 (94.0%) of U.S. organizations.
* Not geocoded, by reason: {'no_zip_in_record': 558, 'non_us_organization': 45}.
* 45 projects are at non-U.S. organizations.
* County assignment: {'zcta_point_in_2024_county': 7766, 'reporter_point_in_2024_county': 973, '(none)': 603}. `zcta_county_rel2020_max_land_share` covers ZCTAs such as 60611 (Chicago), whose internal point lies in Lake Michigan outside the clipped 5m county polygons, so `zcta_centroids` has no county for them. Geocoded rows still without a county: 0.
* ZCTA-geocoded rows whose ZCTA county lies in a different state than `org_state`: 0 (flag `geo_state_matches_org_state`).
* Connecticut (county vintage 2024 = 9 planning regions, 09110-09190): 251 rows have org_state CT and 251 carry a CT county FIPS. 251 of those are 2024 planning regions and 0 are legacy counties (09001-09015). Regions: {'09110': 64, '09120': 1, '09170': 169, '09190': 17}; assignment: {'reporter_point_in_2024_county': 197, 'zcta_point_in_2024_county': 54}. The 2020 ZCTA-county fallback is never applied to CT, because its 2020 counties are not 2024 counties.
* Records without any address: 21 organizations. These are mostly NIH intramural institutes, which RePORTER lists without city or ZIP. They are left ungeocoded rather than placed in Bethesda by assumption. Top: {'NATIONAL INSTITUTE OF ALLERGY AND INFECTIOUS DISEASES': 127, 'NATIONAL INSTITUTE OF NEUROLOGICAL DISORDERS AND STROKE': 76, 'EUNICE KENNEDY SHRIVER NATIONAL INSTITUTE OF CHILD HEALTH & HUMAN DEVELOPMENT': 52, 'NATIONAL INSTITUTE OF MENTAL HEALTH': 42, 'NATIONAL INSTITUTE ON AGING': 35, 'CLINICAL CENTER': 33, 'NATIONAL INSTITUTE OF ENVIRONMENTAL HEALTH SCIENCES': 32, 'NATIONAL CENTER FOR ADVANCING TRANSLATIONAL SCIENCES': 29}.

(CA 94105) 2639; EPIFOCUS LLC (AZ 85259) 1798; INTERO BIOSYSTEMS INC. (CO 80045) 1785; AMERICAN ASSN/CHRONIC FATIGUE SYNDROME (NY 11790) 1214. In these cases RePORTER's point lies in another state from the ZIP on the record, which looks like a stale RePORTER coordinate, so the listed ZIP is primary.

### Linkage strategy
* `nih_projects.appl_id` joins `nih_project_conditions.appl_id` and `nih_project_query_hits.appl_id`.
* `condition_id` joins the ontology layer's condition registry (configs/conditions.yaml ids).
* `county_fips` / `state_fips` / `lat`,`lon` place the **organization** on the map (facility layer). Joining these to geographic burden rows is an ecological, place-level association: it does not mean the grant studies people from that county.
* `org_name` / `org_ipf_code` can be matched to ClinicalTrials.gov sites and NPPES organizations only by fuzzy name + location matching. No shared identifier exists; any such match must be labelled approximate.
* Not joinable: participants (no person-level data); PIs to NPPES NPIs (no shared id).

### Limitations and caveats
* Text-search recall and precision: condition assignment is RePORTER's phrase search over title, abstract and the thesaurus-expanded `terms` field. It includes projects that only mention the condition (e.g. as a comorbidity or exclusion), and misses projects that use other wording. `match_tier` and `rcdc_category_assigned` are measured quality signals; neither is ground truth.
* RCDC categories (measured): only NIH records carry them (0 of 552 non-NIH records); none of the 813 NIH FY2026 records has one yet; category year ranges observed: {'long_covid': 'FY2022-FY2025', 'me_cfs': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'pots': 'FY2020-FY2025', 'fibromyalgia': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'lyme_disease': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'ptlds': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'migraine': 'FY2015-FY2025', 'endometriosis': 'FY2015-FY2025'}. dysautonomia, eds_hsd, mcas, ibs, gastroparesis and post_infectious_syndrome have no condition-specific category, so there is no agreement check for them. The `RCDC assigned` column counts over all fiscal years, including years without the category.
* Funding: `award_amount` is an obligation (total cost) for one fiscal year, not spending; a whole award counts toward every condition it mentions; parent/subproject double counting is removed by `obligation_total`; conditions overlap, so per-condition totals must not be added up.
* FY2026 is incomplete at retrieval.
* Non-NIH agencies that report to RePORTER are included and flagged by `agency_code` ({'NIH': 8790, 'VA': 460, 'AHRQ': 43, 'ALLCDC': 29, 'FDA': 20}). VA records carry no award amount (460 of 460 missing), so VA-heavy organizations show unknown rather than $0 obligations.
* Organization location is the awardee's address. Multi-site studies and subaward sites are not represented.
* Research readiness is not patient burden: counts of grants reflect where research institutions are, which follows population and academic density.

### Processed outputs
| Table | Rows |
|---|---|
| nih_projects | 9,342 |
| nih_project_conditions | 15,358 |
| nih_project_query_hits | 4,696 |

Helper functions for the MCP layer: `projects_for_condition(condition_id)` and `research_centers_for_condition(condition_id)` in `measure_it.ingestion.nih_reporter`.

### Reproduce
`uv run python -m measure_it.ingestion.nih_reporter` (uses the HTTP cache; `--refresh` re-queries the API; `--offline` rebuilds tables from the raw files only).

## Source: nppes

## DATA AUDIT — NPPES NPI Downloadable File (V.2)

> **Mandatory caveat.** An NPI taxonomy code is self-reported and does NOT mean the provider evaluates or treats Long COVID, ME/CFS, POTS or any other invisible illness. NPPES practice addresses are self-reported and may be billing/administrative addresses. lat/lon are ZIP5-as-ZCTA internal points (ZIP centroids), not address geocodes. Deactivated NPIs and non-US practice locations are excluded; only the primary practice location is used.

#### Format version: is there an NPPES V.2? Yes, and it is now the only format

* The CMS listing states that Version 1 of the monthly/weekly files is no longer supported from 03/03/2026 and links only V.2 files. The file used is the **V.2 full replacement monthly file**.
* V.1 reference for comparison: `https://download.cms.gov/nppes/NPPES_Data_Dissemination_February_2026.zip` (last V.1 monthly still hosted; `NPPES_Data_Dissemination_March_2026.zip` returned HTTP 404 when probed on 2026-09-23). Only its header CSV and README were read, by HTTP range requests (1,273,619 bytes of 1,109,383,607); copies in `data/raw/nppes/v1_reference/`.
* Data-file header: V.1 330 columns, V.2 330 columns; column names identical and in the same order: **True**. (Both include `Healthcare Provider Taxonomy Group_1..15` and `Certification Date`.)
* README max-length differences (V.1 README → V.2 README; parsed with pdftotext):
  * Data File Layout :: Authorized Official First Name: 20 → 35
  * Data File Layout :: NPI Deactivation Reason Code: 2 → not publicly disseminated (no length given)
  * Data File Layout :: Parent Organization LBN: 70 → 100

* `NPI Deactivation Reason Code`: non-blank values in the V.2 data file: 0.
* Measured impact in the current V.2 file — values longer than the V.1 limit (these would have been truncated in V.1): Provider First Name > 20: 230; Provider Organization Name (Legal Business Name) > 70: 345; Provider Other First Name > 20: 32; Provider Other Organization Name > 70: 0; Authorized Official First Name > 20: 42; Parent Organization LBN > 70: 8.

* NPI rows in file: 9,798,758 (NPI unique: True).

### Specialty and facility groups

Defined in `configs/specialty_groups.yaml` (hand-chosen NUCC codes with verbatim NUCC display names; checked against the NUCC file by `tests/test_nppes.py`). Key ambiguity notes:

* **primary_care** — Physicians: family medicine, general practice, general internal medicine, general pediatrics, and their geriatric/adolescent/adult-medicine specialisations. Nurse practitioners are included ONLY when their NUCC population focus is primary-care-like (family, primary care, adult health, gerontology, pediatrics, community health). Generic NP (363L00000X) and all physician-assistant codes are excluded because NUCC does not encode their specialty. Use the role / n_individual_physicians columns to restrict to physicians. NOTE: 207R00000X Internal Medicine is also the base code many hospital-based internists and subspecialists carry, so primary_care counts are an upper bound on outpatient primary care.
* **electrophysiology** — Cardiac electrophysiology only (the SPEC context is autonomic/ECG measurement). Neurophysiology codes (2084N0600X Clinical Neurophysiology, 204R00000X Electrodiagnostic Medicine) are placed in neurology, not here. NUCC has no separate pediatric EP code.
* **neurology** — Psychiatry & Neurology classifications that are neurological, plus Electrodiagnostic Medicine. Clinical Neurophysiology is included because its NUCC definition covers 'central, peripheral, and autonomic nervous system disorders' diagnosed with electrophysiologic testing; Electrodiagnostic Medicine is ambiguous (its NUCC definition covers neurologic, neuromuscular and muscular impairments, a field shared with PM&R) and is placed here only.
* **vascular** — Vascular surgery, phlebology (venous disease) and vascular & interventional radiology. NUCC has NO vascular-medicine physician code: vascular-medicine specialists register as Cardiovascular Disease or Internal Medicine and cannot be separated from those groups.
* **pmr** — Every NUCC code whose classification is Physical Medicine & Rehabilitation (physiatrists). Physical and occupational therapists are NOT included (they are not physicians; see DATA_AUDIT for their measured NPI counts).
* **clinical_research** — The only research-specific NUCC codes: Research Clinic/Center (organisations) and Research Study Specialist (individuals). Very few NPIs use them; research readiness should come from ClinicalTrials.gov and NIH RePORTER, not from these codes.

### Key variables (table `providers`)

| column | meaning | used downstream for |
|---|---|---|
| npi | 10-digit National Provider Identifier (string) | key; `source_record_id` |
| entity_type / entity_type_label | 1 individual, 2 organisation | individual vs facility counts |
| name, provider_last_name, provider_first_name, organization_name, credential | legal names; credential is free text (individuals only) | display |
| primary_taxonomy_code (+ NUCC display/grouping/classification/specialization) | taxonomy flagged primary | primary specialty |
| all_taxonomy_codes | all distinct codes (pipe-separated) | audit |
| specialty_groups / primary_specialty_groups / group_types | groups matched by any / primary code | clinic matching (`clinical_specialty_match`) |
| address_line1, city, state, zip5, phone | primary practice location (self-reported) | display, geocoding |
| lat, lon, county_fips, geocode_method | ZIP5-as-ZCTA internal point and containing 2024 county | maps, radius search, county density |
| enumeration_date, last_update_date, deactivation_date, reactivation_date, certification_date | NPPES dates | record staleness |
| n_secondary_practice_locations | rows in the practice-location reference file | flags multi-site providers |

Other outputs: `provider_specialty_groups` (NPI × group, with primary flag and matched codes), `provider_density_county` (2024 county × group counts incl. zeros), `provider_taxonomy_counts` (national counts per group and per considered taxonomy code), `nucc_taxonomy`.

* Geocoded (ZIP5 matches a 2024 ZCTA): 1,434,874 (98.53%). Not geocoded by reason: {'zip5_not_a_2024_zcta': 21471, 'no_5_digit_zip': 1} (PO-box/unique ZIPs have no ZCTA).
* Ungeocoded providers are concentrated in a few non-ZCTA ZIP5s (typically single-institution unique ZIPs), so county specialist counts are biased LOW in the counties that hold them. Top 10 (ZIP5: NPIs, modal city/state): 44195: 2,379 (CLEVELAND OH); 94143: 1,928 (SAN FRANCISCO CA); 68198: 970 (OMAHA NE); 03756: 918 (LEBANON NH); 27157: 891 (WINSTON SALEM NC); 27710: 867 (DURHAM NC); 84132: 823 (SALT LAKE CITY UT); 01655: 696 (WORCESTER MA); 76508: 663 (TEMPLE TX); 65212: 656 (COLUMBIA MO). Ungeocoded share by SPEC group: cardiology 2.3%, electrophysiology 2.8%, neurology 3.3%, rheumatology 2.4%, immunology_allergy 1.5%, infectious_disease 3.4%, pulmonology 2.9%, gastroenterology 2.1%, pain_medicine 1.2%, pmr 1.0%, primary_care 1.4%, gynecology 1.3%, vascular 2.3%.
* County assignment among geocoded rows: {'zcta_point_in_2024_county': 1434874}. `zcta_largest_land_share_rel2020` = the ZCTA's Census internal point lies outside every 2024 county polygon (coastal ZCTAs whose point falls in water, e.g. 60611 Chicago), so the county with the largest land share in the 2020 ZCTA-county relationship file is used (only if it is a 2024 county). ZIPs affected: {}.
* Geocoded rows whose assigned county lies in a different state than the address state: 500 (0.03%).
* **Connecticut vintage.** Counties are the 2024 vintage (CT = 9 planning regions 09110-09190); the relationship-file fallback only assigns 2024 counties. Measured: 17,215 providers with a CT address; 16,226 placed in a 2024 planning region, 0 on a legacy CT county (09001-09015), 2 in another state's county (ZIP/state mismatch), 987 not geocoded. Any provider on a legacy CT county: False. Tables on legacy CT counties (e.g. CMS) will not join `provider_density_county` for CT; report, do not force a mapping.
* Geocoded NPIs whose county is not in the 2024 county list: 0 counties ([]).
* Record staleness — last_update_date quantiles (10/25/50/75/90%) over all kept US NPIs: ['2009-02-26', '2014-12-12', '2021-05-30', '2024-10-14', '2026-02-12']. Old dates mean the record has not been touched, not that it is wrong, but addresses may be out of date.
* Providers with ≥1 secondary practice location: 241,309 (16.57%).

### Linkage strategy

* `npi` joins `providers` ↔ `provider_specialty_groups`; `county_fips` (2024 vintage) joins `provider_density_county` to `geographies` and to county-level geographic tables — an **ecological** join.
* NPIs can be joined to other NPI-keyed public sources (e.g. CMS utilisation files) but NOT to any person-level participant data: providers are not participants, and nothing here says which patients a provider sees.
* ClinicalTrials.gov / RePORTER sites carry no NPI; matching them to NPPES organisations would be fuzzy name/address matching and must be labelled as such.

### Limitations and caveats

* An NPI taxonomy code is self-reported and does NOT mean the provider evaluates or treats Long COVID, ME/CFS, POTS or any other invisible illness. NPPES practice addresses are self-reported and may be billing/administrative addresses. lat/lon are ZIP5-as-ZCTA internal points (ZIP centroids), not address geocodes. Deactivated NPIs and non-US practice locations are excluded; only the primary practice location is used.
* Taxonomy codes are self-selected and need not be kept current (median last_update_date of kept US NPIs: 2021-05-30); a code shows self-description, not current practice, capacity, or acceptance of new patients.
* Counts are NPIs, not FTEs: an individual clinician and the organisations they work for each hold NPIs, and organisations can register subparts (e.g. hospital departments) as separate NPIs (40,502 (10.97%) of organisations in `providers` are subparts). A practice address can be an administrative/billing address.
* The primary-care group includes 207R00000X Internal Medicine, which is also carried by hospital-based and subspecialty internists, so it is an upper bound on outpatient primary care. Of 270,375 NPIs carrying 207R00000X Internal Medicine: 28,930 (10.70%) also carry 208M00000X Hospitalist, 39,026 (14.43%) also match another group (a subspecialty or facility code), and 41,651 (15.40%) have a primary taxonomy outside every group. Nurse practitioners are included only for primary-care population foci and physician assistants are excluded (NUCC does not encode PA specialty).
* Facility groups are self-reported NPPES taxonomies, not certification rosters (use HRSA for FQHC sites, CMS certification files for RHCs/CAHs).
* ZIP-centroid geocoding can place a practice in the wrong county for ZIPs that cross county lines; PO-box/unique ZIPs are not geocoded.
* Territories are included; ZCTA coverage there is limited (not geocoded: territories 1,175 (7.52%) vs 50 states + DC 20,297 (1.41%)).

| table | rows |

| providers | 1,456,346 |
| provider_specialty_groups | 1,556,031 |

| provider_taxonomy_counts | 184 |

### Reproduce

`uv run python -m measure_it.ingestion.nppes` (add `--refresh-listing` to pick up a newer monthly file). Stats: `data/raw/nppes/ingest_stats.json`; format comparison: `data/raw/nppes/format_version_comparison.json`.

Previous versions linked on the page: nucc_taxonomy_251.csv, nucc_taxonomy_250.csv, nucc_taxonomy_241.csv, nucc_taxonomy_240.csv, nucc_taxonomy_231.csv, nucc_taxonomy_230.csv …

### Key variables

Code (10 chars) → Grouping → Classification → Specialization; Display Name; Definition; Notes; Section (Individual / Non-Individual). Used to name NPPES taxonomy codes and to build `configs/specialty_groups.yaml`; `role` is derived (physician / nurse_practitioner / physician_assistant / other_individual / facility).

### Linkage strategy

Joins to NPPES on the taxonomy code. Coverage: of 873 distinct codes used by active US NPPES records, codes absent from this NUCC version: {'246ZS0400X': {'npi_code_pairs': 4, 'as_primary': 2}}.

### Limitations and caveats

* A taxonomy is a self-selected provider classification, not a credential, board certification, or statement of which conditions a provider treats.
* Many facility codes have no definition ('Definition to come...').
* Deactivated codes are not in the current CSV; NPPES records may still carry them (see coverage above).

| Source version / release | Open Targets Platform data release 26.06; API 26.6.3 (meta.name: Open Targets GraphQL & REST API Beta) |
| Source update date / cadence | quarterly Platform data releases (release above is the one served at retrieval) |
| License / access conditions | open API, no key; Open Targets Platform data are released under CC0 1.0 |
| Unit of observation | target (gene) x disease association; evidence item; drug x disease indication; Reactome pathway annotation of associated targets |
| Sample size (actual, as ingested) | 52164 rows: 29152 association rows (11864 unique targets), 1895 top-evidence rows, 643 drug rows, 20474 pathway rows |
| Geography (resolution, vintage) | none (concept-level) |
| Person-level? | no |
| Geographic? | no |
| Omics? | yes — condition-level molecular evidence only |
| Wearable? | no |
| True participant linkage across modalities? | no — disease-concept level; no participants |

### Files / endpoints retrieved

Every GraphQL response is saved under `data/raw/open_targets/` (meta.json, diseases/, associations/, evidence/, drugs/, target_pathways/, datasource_probe/) and listed with sha256, bytes and the GraphQL variables in MANIFEST.json. `run_log.json` holds the measured datasource->datatype map, the evidence retrieval log and the descendant check. Responses are also cached by `measure_it.http`.

### Descendant diseases: does the association call include them by default?

Measured per id: the association count with the API default equals the count with `enableIndirect: true` and exceeds the direct-only count wherever the disease has descendants. The default call is therefore **indirect (descendants included)**; the partition keeps the default scores and adds `direct_association_score` / `has_direct_association` from the direct-only call.

Pagination (measured): paging the API's default score-descending order across the 3000-row page boundary repeated some targets and skipped others, reproducibly (e.g. endometriosis 3561 rows but 3521 unique targets). Ids with more than 3000 associations are paged with `orderByScore: "score asc"`, which returned every target exactly once, and re-sorted by score; the module fails if the retrieved unique targets differ from the API count.

### Per-condition counts

`unique_targets` counts every target associated with any registry id of the condition (including descendants and broad/component ids); `unique_targets_exact_or_narrow_ids` restricts to ids whose registry predicate is exact or narrow (a broad primary id such as post-infectious disorder is excluded). `unique_targets_primary_id` / `genetic_association_targets_primary_id` restrict to the condition's registry primary id. `direct_targets` = targets with a direct (non-propagated) association. Genetic-association targets = targets with a non-zero `genetic_association` datatype score.

### Datatypes and datasources (measured)

Datasource -> datatype map measured from one evidence item per datasource: `cancer_gene_census`->`somatic_mutation`, `clingen`->`genetic_literature`, `clinical_precedence`->`clinical`, `europepmc`->`literature`, `eva`->`genetic_association`, `eva_somatic`->`somatic_mutation`, `expression_atlas`->`rna_expression`, `gene2phenotype`->`genetic_literature`, `gene_burden`->`genetic_association`, `genomics_england`->`genetic_literature`, `gwas_credible_sets`->`genetic_association`, `impc`->`animal_model`, `intogen`->`somatic_mutation`, `orphanet`->`genetic_association`, `reactome`->`affected_pathway`, `uniprot_literature`->`genetic_literature`, `uniprot_variants`->`genetic_association`.

Requested datatype -> API datatype used: genetic_association->genetic_association, genetic_literature->genetic_literature, known_drug->clinical, literature->literature, rna_expression->rna_expression, animal_model->animal_model, affected_pathway->affected_pathway. In this release the drug datatype is named `clinical` (datasource `clinical_precedence`); the `knownDrugs` field no longer exists and `drugAndClinicalCandidates` replaces it.

### Key variables

* `source_score` — association rows: Open Targets overall association score (0-1, source-provided, label 'Open Targets association score (source-provided)'); evidence rows: the evidence item score (source-provided). Drug and pathway rows have no source score (null).
* `ot_datatype_score__<datatype>` — per-datatype association scores exactly as returned (genetic_association, genetic_literature, somatic_mutation, clinical, affected_pathway, rna_expression, literature, animal_model); null = the datatype contributed no evidence.
* `ot_datasource_scores_json` — per-datasource scores as returned.
* `direct_association_score`, `has_direct_association` — from the `enableIndirect:false` call.
* evidence rows: `ot_datasource`, `evidence_disease_id` (the disease the evidence is annotated to; `evidence_from_descendant`), `study_id`, `variant_rsids`, `p_value`, `odds_ratio`, `beta`, `literature_pmids`, `drug_id`, `clinical_stage`, `reactome_id`, `evidence_direction` (`target:GoF/LoF`, `trait:risk/protect`, only where Open Targets defines it).
* drug rows: `max_clinical_stage_for_indication`, `drug_maximum_clinical_stage_any_indication`, `mechanism_of_action`, `action_types`, `mechanism_target_symbols`, `trial_status_counts`, `reports_cite_queried_id` (false = the indication reaches the queried id through a related disease in the clinical reports).
* pathway rows: `n_associated_targets_annotated`, `n_genetic_association_targets_annotated`, `top_target_symbols`, `reactome_top_level_term` (annotation counts, no enrichment test).

### Linkage strategy

Registry condition -> MONDO/EFO ids from `condition_registry.mondo_ids`/`efo_ids` (ontology module) -> Open Targets disease id (same CURIE with '_' separator). No text matching and no guessed ids: an id absent from the Open Targets disease index is recorded as 'identifier not present in source'. Targets join to other tables by Ensembl gene id only. Nothing here is joinable to participants.

### Limitations and caveats

* Associations are indirect by default: a broad id (e.g. autonomic nervous system disorder, Ehlers-Danlos syndrome, post-infectious disorder) pulls in every descendant disease, including monogenic subtypes and neoplasms (see the measured table of evidence diseases above). `ontology_match`/`ontology_id_role`, `unique_targets_primary_id` and `has_direct_association` must be used before interpreting a condition's target list.
* Literature (Europe PMC text mining) and animal-model (IMPC) evidence dominate target counts for poorly characterised conditions; they are co-mention/phenotype-similarity signals, not mechanism.
* Open Targets scores are relative, source-specific heuristics; they are not probabilities and must not be combined with GWAS/GEO counts into a single score.
* For POTS the only Open Targets id is MONDO_0011479, which the registry/Mondo labels 'POTS due to NET deficiency' (a monogenic form, predicate narrow) while Open Targets labels it 'postural orthostatic tachycardia syndrome'.
* `drugAndClinicalCandidates` is not documented as ontology-propagated; `reports_cite_queried_id` records whether the linked clinical reports name the queried id itself.
* Condition-level molecular enrichment/evidence only — never patient multi-omics.

### Processed outputs

* `condition_molecular_evidence__open_targets`: 52164 rows
| evidence_type | entity_type | rows |
|---|---|---|
| genetic_association | gene | 444 |
| known_drug | drug | 643 |
| known_drug | gene | 439 |
| pathway_annotation | gene | 3 |
| pathway_annotation | pathway | 20474 |
| target_disease_association | gene | 29494 |
| text_mined | gene | 667 |
* `condition_molecular_coverage__open_targets`: 27 rows
* `condition_molecular_evidence` / `condition_molecular_coverage`: union of all partitions (store.union_partitions)

### Reproduce

`uv run python -m measure_it.omics.open_targets`

<a id="source-openfda-device"></a>

## Source: openfda_device

## DATA AUDIT — openFDA device endpoints (classification, 510(k) incl. De Novo, PMA, registration & listing)

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| source_id | `openfda_device` |
| Source (dataset/API name, exact files/endpoints) | `https://api.fda.gov/device/classification.json`, `/510k.json`, `/pma.json`, `/registrationlisting.json`; bulk file `https://download.open.fda.gov/device/classification/device-classification-0001-of-0001.json.zip` |
| Publishing organization | U.S. Food and Drug Administration (openFDA) |
| Retrieval date (UTC) | 2026-09-23 (API calls 20:35:52–21:00:50 UTC; the last 8 calls, at 21:00:50, re-fetched the two re-curated KTB/INQ units; bulk classification file 20:35:52 UTC) |
| Source version / release | API `meta.last_updated` = 2026-09-14 on all four endpoints; bulk classification file `meta.last_updated` = 2026-09-23 (HTTP Last-Modified Wed, 23 Sep 2026 19:01:14 GMT) |
| Source update date / cadence | API data `last_updated` 2026-09-14; the download index (`https://api.fda.gov/download.json`, checked 2026-09-23) listed device export_date 2026-09-23. openFDA refreshes these datasets periodically; the cadence was not verified here |
| License / access conditions | Public domain, dedicated under Creative Commons CC0 1.0 Universal (https://open.fda.gov/license/); terms at https://open.fda.gov/terms/. Open API, no key used; limits without a key are 240 requests/min and 1,000 requests/day per IP (https://open.fda.gov/apis/authentication/) |
| Unit of observation | FDA product code (classification); one 510(k) or De Novo decision; one PMA original or supplement; per-product-code counts of registration & listing records |
| Sample size (actual, as ingested) | 90 mapped product codes (98 measurement→code mappings across 22 of 26 measurement classes; 4 classes with no code); 8,101 510(k)/De Novo records (8,085 K, 16 DEN); 132 PMA records (13 PMA numbers); 95 registration-count rows; 456 named-device search hits; 576 logged API calls |
| Geography (resolution, vintage) | none. Applicant addresses are submitter addresses, not deployment sites; nothing is geocoded |
| Person-level? | no |
| Geographic? | no |
| Omics? | no (regulatory records about some omics assay categories; no omics data) |
| Wearable? | no (regulatory records about wearable device categories; no wearable data) |
| True participant linkage across modalities? | no — regulatory records contain no people |

Whole-endpoint totals at retrieval (recorded queries, `api/endpoint_totals.json`): classification 7,093 product codes; 510k 176,140 records, of which 489 are De Novo (`k_number:DEN*`); PMA 57,137 records; registrationlisting 335,617 records.

### Files / endpoints retrieved

* `device-classification-0001-of-0001.json.zip` — 3,277,559 bytes, sha256 `69b86328b11ffba946f0f861fe5fb53bc91d3669f7b669bd2e4fb2955dca0916` (MANIFEST.json). 7,093 product codes. Used as FDA's authoritative list: every mapped code must exist in it and its `device_name` must equal the name in the curated map.
* `QUERY_LOG.json` — every API call (576): endpoint, params, HTTP status (404 = "No matches found!", 172 calls), `meta.results.total`, rows returned, `last_updated`, fetch time, purpose, and the product codes each discovery query returned. Same content as the processed table `fda_device_query_log`. Also lists sha256/bytes of the 288 saved response files (69.7 MB).
* `api/classification_discovery.json` — full classification records returned by 130 discovery queries (keywords per measurement class, each searched in `device_name` OR `definition`, `limit=1000`).
* `api/named_device_searches.json` — full 510k records returned by 59 named-device/applicant searches.
* `api/510k/<code>[__f<hash>].json` — per product code (and device filter), `search=product_code:<code>[ AND <filter>]&sort=decision_date:desc&limit=1000`.
* `api/pma/*.json` — `pma.json?search=product_code:<code>&limit=1000`.
* `api/registrationlisting/*.json` — `count=registration.registration_number&limit=1000` for `products.product_code:<code>` (all countries and `AND registration.iso_country_code:US`); for filtered generic codes the search is restricted to listings carrying the filtered K/DEN/P numbers (`k_number:(...)`).
* `discovery/classification_candidates.csv`, `discovery/named_device_hits.csv` — human-readable candidate lists used for curation.

De Novo: openFDA has no De Novo endpoint (`/device/denovo.json` → HTTP 404). De Novo grants are in `/device/510k.json` with `DEN` numbers and `decision_code` `DENG` (their `decision_description` is "Unknown" in openFDA).

### Method

1. **Discovery.** `DISCOVERY_TERMS` in the module lists 130 keywords derived from each class's name, signals and text patterns in `configs/measurements.yaml`. 36 of them returned zero product codes (e.g. actigraph\*, "heart rate variability", holter, tilt, sudomotor, valsalva, "skin biopsy", "reactive hyperemia", "flow-mediated", capillaroscop\*, nailfold, "laser doppler", gait, cytokine, "d-dimer", metabolom\*, proteom\*, transcriptom\*). Because several relevant devices sit under generically named codes, 59 named-device searches on `510k.json` (device_name / applicant) were also run and recorded.
2. **Curation** (human): `configs/fda_product_code_map.yaml`. A code is kept only if its FDA `device_name`/`definition` describes the measurement, or, for a generic code, if the named-device records under it do. In that case the code is mapped only with a `device_filter` and every count for that row is restricted to the filter (12 filtered rows: GWQ, ISD, DPS×2, DRT, BZM, BTY, KTB, INQ, JOM, MXK, DQK). `mapping_confidence`: 29 high, 43 medium, 26 low, plus 4 `no specific product code found` entries. On every run, `validate_code_map()` rejects a code that is not in the bulk classification file, whose name differs from FDA's, or that its `query_that_found_it` did not return.
3. **Records.** For each (code, filter) unit (95 units), all 510k records are kept when total ≤ 1,000 (93 units). Otherwise the 100 most recent are kept, and the De Novo count comes from a separate query (IYN: 100 of 1,183; LNH: 100 of 1,105). `record_retrieval_mode` records which. PMA records and registration counts were fetched for every unit.

### Key variables

* `measurement_regulatory_status` (one row per measurement × code [× filter]):
  * `n_510k` / `n_denovo`: K-number / DEN-number decisions under the code (restricted by `device_filter` when set).
  * `n_pma`: PMA original approvals. `n_pma_supplements`: supplement records. `n_pma_numbers_any_record`: distinct PMA numbers.
  * `n_registered_establishments(_us)`: distinct establishment registration numbers whose listings carry the code.
  * `most_recent_decision_date`, and `example_devices`, a JSON list of up to 8 K/DEN/P numbers taken only from the retrieved records.
  * `mapping_confidence`, `mapping_rationale`, `query_that_found_it`.
  * `regulatory_visibility`: `decisions_on_record` / `category_exists_no_decisions_in_openfda` / `no_product_code_found`.
  * `regulatory_note`.
  * Downstream use: the `regulatory_visibility` / `technology_maturity` component and the MCP tool `get_regulatory_context` (`measure_it.measurements.regulatory`).
* `fda_510k`: `clearance_id` (K/DEN), `submission_kind`, `product_code`, `device_name`, `applicant`, `decision_date` (YYYY-MM-DD), `decision_code`/`decision_description`, `clearance_type`, `device_filters`, `mapped_measurement_ids`.
* `fda_pma`: `pma_number`, `supplement_number`, `is_original`, `trade_name`, `generic_name`, `applicant`, `decision_date`.
* `fda_registration_listing_counts`: `n_device_listings`, `n_establishments`, `n_establishments_us`, `n_establishments_non_us`.
* `fda_named_device_findings`: every hit of the named searches. `record_counted_in_status` is True when the record falls inside a mapped unit. Unrelated products of the same applicant are False, e.g. Withings blood-pressure monitors (DXN) and AliveCor KardiaAI (DQK).

### Missingness (measured)

`fda_510k` (8,101 rows): device_name, applicant, decision_date, date_received, decision_code, clearance_type and applicant_country_code are 0 % missing. `statement_or_summary` is missing for 2,321 rows (28.7 %). `applicant_state` is missing for 1,933 (23.9 %), mostly non-US applicants: all 1,931 non-US rows lack it, plus 2 US rows. `expedited_review_flag` is missing for 8,100 (100.0 %). Decisions span 1976-07-19 to 2026-09-11. Decision codes: SESE 8,059; DENG 16; ST 14; SN 7; SESK 3; SESP 2. All are substantially-equivalent variants or De Novo grants; openFDA does not publish NSE decisions here.

`fda_device_classification` (90 rows): `definition` is empty for 54 (60.0 %; older codes have none). `regulation_number` is empty for 3 (3.3 %: MAN, QLC and the unclassified LQD). `submission_type`: 78 510(k), 8 510(k)-exempt, 1 PMA; 3 carry values the openFDA field spec does not document (7 for OCH and PGJ, 8 for QLC).

`fda_pma` (132 rows): trade_name and decision_date are 0 % missing; `supplement_type` is missing for 46 (34.8 %). Only IYN (1 original + 2 supplements), MAN (1 + 1) and LNH (0 originals, 127 supplements) have PMA records. The LNH records are 1980s supplements to MRI PMAs (e.g. P830053, P830074, P830076, P840063) whose original approvals are not returned under LNH.

`measurement_regulatory_status` (102 rows): the 4 no-code rows have NA counts, by design ("unknown", not zero). Among the 98 coded rows, all counts are present. 5 coded rows have a category but no decisions in openFDA (0 510(k), 0 De Novo, 0 PMA): OCH, PGJ, PJZ, PTY and QLC. QVT, QOK and SAX have only a De Novo grant (DEN200011, DEN200038 and DEN230043) and count as `decisions_on_record`. `n_registered_establishments` is 0 for 7 units: DPS[HRV], DPS[autonomic], INQ["tilt table"], HCS, MAN, QOK, QVT. The cleared devices exist, but no current listing carries the code or the filtered K numbers.

### Measurement classes (measured result)

* Classes with a mapped FDA category: 22 of 26.
* **No specific product code found**: `posture_detection`, `capillaroscopy`, `metabolomics`, `proteomics`. The queries tried are in the YAML and in the table's `query_that_found_it`.
* `digital_cognitive` has an FDA category, contrary to what one might assume: PKQ "Computerized Cognitive Assessment Aid" (DEN130033 COGNIVUE, K150154 ANAM, K161328 Cantab Mobile), PTY (exempt counterpart), and POM for concussion (DEN150037 ImPACT + 7 × 510(k)).
* HRV, endothelial function, sudomotor testing and tilt tables have **no dedicated code**. They appear only through generic codes with device filters (low confidence).
* Omics: transcriptomics maps only to disease-specific IVD categories: PRE host-response RT-qPCR; OJQ AlloMap (DEN080007, K221640); NYI MammaPrint/Prosigna.

#### Devices looked for specifically (numbers from the API, `fda_named_device_findings` / `fda_510k`)

* **Consumer wearable ECG (QDA, "Electrocardiograph Software For Over-The-Counter Use")**: DEN180044 Apple ECG App (2018-09-11); K201525 Apple ECG App; K200948 Fitbit ECG App; K201168, K230292, K240909 Samsung; K221774 Garmin ECG App; K243236 WHOOP ECG Feature; K240795 Withings ECG App (2025-06-15). 8 × 510(k) + 1 De Novo.
* **Irregular-rhythm (PPG) features (QDB)**: DEN180042 Apple IRNF (2018-09-11); K212516, K213971 (AFib History), K231173 Apple; K212372 Fitbit Irregular Rhythm Notifications. SDY loss-of-pulse: K242967 Fitbit.
* **Other consumer-wearable features found**: QZW sleep-apnea risk notification (DEN230041 and K261011 Samsung; K240929 Apple); QZA body-temperature software (DEN230050, K243778 Fitbit); AliveCor Kardia Band System K171816 (DXH).
* **No records**: `applicant:google` and `applicant:oura` returned no 510(k)/De Novo records.
* **Wearable patch ECG (Zio)**: DSH K090363, K113862, K121319, K143513, K202359, K243650; DSI K240177; QYX K163512, K240029. iRhythm ZEUS software sits under generic DQK (e.g. K202527) and is not counted. Bardy: 7 DSH records, all "Carnation Ambulatory Monitor" (e.g. K143067, K210036).
* **Peripheral arterial tonometry**: K001852 PAT 1000RD, K032519 ENDO PAT 2000, K211557 EndoPATx, all under generic DQK "Computer, Diagnostic, Programmable". VENDYS (K060980, K072919) is also under DQK. WatchPAT (sleep) is under MNR (17 Itamar MNR records).
* **Sudomotor testing**: K100233 and K141872 SUDOSCAN (Impeto) under GZO "Device, Galvanic Skin Response Measurement". Other GZO sudomotor-named devices include SUDO PATH K131568 and SudoC K152216. GZO is mapped unfiltered (medium), but only 4 of its 26 records are sudomotor-named. Most of the rest are electrodermal "meridian"/EAV screening devices (e.g. MSAS, AKUPORT, Asyra, ZYTO Hand Cradle, QBioScan) cleared as galvanic-skin-response measurement devices. Q-Sweat K992874 is under KTB (iontophoresis), which is counted only for `device_name:"quantitative sweat"`. The other five KTB `sweat` records (Macroduct, Nanoduct, CF Indicator, Webster sweat inducer, iontophoretic sweat-chloride stimulator) are cystic-fibrosis sweat-chloride tests, not sudomotor tests, and are excluded. `device_name:sudomotor` and `device_name:qsart` returned 0.
* **Tilt tables**: no dedicated code. INQ "Table, Powered" (physical medicine) is counted only for device names containing "tilt table": 9 records, 1984-1993, none stating autonomic testing, and 0 current listings. The one "ELECTROPHYSIOLOGY TILT TABLE" (K965105) is under IXR "Table, Radiographic, Tilting" and is not mapped. It is recorded in `fda_named_device_findings` only.
* **Actigraphy**: LEL K181077 ActiGraph CentrePoint Insight Watch, K231532 ActiGraph LEAP. Under GWQ (EEG code): K854030 wrist actigraph, K040554 Actigraph AM7164, K983533 Actiwatch, K991033 Actiwatch-Score. ISD: K080545 ActiTrainer. `geneactiv` and `motionwatch` returned 0.
* **Nailfold capillaroscopy**: none. capillaroscop\*, videocapillaroscop\*, nailfold, "capillary microscope", cytocam and "dark field" all returned 0 510(k)/De Novo records. The classification terms capillaroscop\*, nailfold, dermatoscope and microcirculation returned 0 codes. "microscope" and "capillary" returned only surgical, laboratory and ophthalmic microscope codes and blood-collection/dialysis codes.

### Audit corrections (independent audit, 2026-09-23)

* KTB device filter narrowed from `device_name:sweat` to `device_name:"quantitative sweat"`. The broad filter had counted five cystic-fibrosis sweat-chloride systems as autonomic/sudomotor testing, so KTB n_510k went from 6 to 1 (Q-Sweat K992874).
* INQ device filter narrowed from `device_name:tilt` to `device_name:"tilt table"`, the query that found the code. This removed a tilt bed, an obstetric table and three tilting exam/treatment tables, taking n_510k from 14 to 9.
* `fda_510k` went from 8,111 to 8,101 rows as a result. Stale per-unit response files are now pruned when the curated map changes.
* `get_regulatory_context`: matching layers are now applied in order, not unioned. Before, "respiratory rate" also returned HRV/ECG/PPG/autonomic classes through the shared word "rate". `exclude_patterns` now block a class in every layer (before, "sleep questionnaire" matched objective sleep). Fuzzy matching no longer applies to 4-letter tokens: "Q-Sweat" had matched "The Heart Seat", and "heat" had matched "heart".
* Registration searches put PMA numbers in `pma_number`, not `k_number`. No filtered unit currently has PMA records, so no count changed.
* `registry_entry.yaml` `retrieved_at` and the no-code rows' `retrieved_at` are now the actual API fetch time, not the rebuild time.

### Linkage strategy

Join key = FDA `product_code` (and `device_filter` for generic codes). `measurement_id` comes from the curated YAML, which bridges to `configs/measurements.yaml` and hence to the measurement layer. K/DEN/P numbers link `fda_510k`/`fda_pma` to registration listings. Nothing here joins to people or places. Applicant and registration addresses are firm addresses: they are **not** deployment sites or clinics, and must not be used as geographic evidence of technology availability.

### Limitations and caveats

* A regulatory record is a deployment-readiness signal, not evidence that the technology diagnoses the target illness. No record here concerns Long COVID, ME/CFS, POTS or similar illnesses.
* The product-code → measurement mapping is a human curation of FDA category names. Categories are broad (e.g. DSH also holds cough monitors, electronic stethoscopes and ambulatory BP recorders: of 195 DSH records, 88 name Holter/ECG/cardiac/rhythm/Zio or are iRhythm/Bardy). Counts are category counts, not counts of devices validated for the measurement.
* Generic codes are included only with device filters, and a device-name filter misses devices named differently (e.g. an HRV module without "HRV" in its name).
* **Exemptions.** Eight mapped codes are 510(k)-exempt per classification: GZO, INQ, ISD, KHX, LLN, LQW, PJZ and PTY. Exempt and unclassified categories can have marketed devices with no 510(k): PTY, PJZ, OCH and PGJ have 42, 53, 59 and 38 listing establishments respectively, with zero 510(k) records. Conversely, GZO is flagged exempt yet has 26 510(k) records.
* Consumer wellness wearables without medical claims (step counts, sleep scores, resting heart rate) are generally not FDA-cleared and are invisible here.
* **Laboratory-developed tests.** LDTs are not in these endpoints: most omics, GPCR autoantibodies, microclot and skin-biopsy IENFD tests.
* IYN and LNH counts are exact, but only their 100 most recent records are stored.
* The De Novo coverage of the 510k endpoint (489 DEN records in total) was not checked against FDA's separate De Novo database, which has no openFDA endpoint.
* Registration counts depend on current listings. Older clearances whose products are no longer listed contribute 0.
* openFDA disclaims: "Do not rely on openFDA to make decisions regarding medical care... assume all results are unvalidated."

### Processed outputs

| table | rows |
|---|---|
| `fda_device_classification` | 90 |
| `fda_510k` | 8,101 |
| `fda_pma` | 132 |
| `fda_registration_listing_counts` | 95 |
| `measurement_regulatory_status` | 102 |
| `fda_named_device_findings` | 456 (320 counted in a mapped unit) |
| `fda_device_query_log` | 576 |

### Reproduce

`uv run python -m measure_it.ingestion.openfda` (use `--discover-only` to rerun only the discovery queries for re-curation). API responses are cached under `data/_http_cache`. A fresh run makes about 580 openFDA calls, which is within the 1,000/day anonymous limit.

### Files / endpoints retrieved

### Key variables

| column | meaning | downstream use |
|---|---|---|
| evidence_id | `PDE-###`; `object_id` = `published_evidence:PDE-###` | traceability |
| condition_ids | `;`-list of `condition_registry` ids the claim is about | query filter |
| condition_definition_in_study | the label as the study defined it (e.g. "RECOVER LCRI", "Fukuda", "self-reported") | label strength |
| device_or_test | device/test in the study's words | text fallback in the query |
| measurement_classes | `;`-list of `configs/measurements.yaml` class ids (empty when no class fits) | query filter |
| measurement_class_gap | why no class fits (pupillometry, voice, gastric motility, pelvic imaging, bowel sound) | registry gap list |

| finding_direction | positive / null / mixed / not_applicable, as the device-vs-condition claim reads | nulls reported beside positives |
| study_design, evidence_tier | design as read; tier = pooled (meta-analysis / systematic review) / large_primary (>= ~100 cases) / small_primary / definition | quality |
| n_cases, n_controls, count_derivation | counts; `count_derivation` says when a count was summed or taken from this project rather than read | never filled silently |
| control_group, reference_standard, population_setting | spectrum and reference | risk of bias |
| metric, metric_value, ci_or_spread, auroc, sensitivity, specificity | the result as read | — |
| external_validation | yes / no / not_applicable | 0 of 17 diagnostic-accuracy rows report external validation |
| risk_of_bias_notes | curator's notes: spectrum bias, incorporation bias, in-sample thresholds, leakage, tiny control groups | — |
| source_quote, quote_source | verbatim quote segments (joined by ` \|\| `) and which text they come from | anti-fabrication check |
| quote_verified, numbers_traced_to_quote, doi_matches_source | build-time checks (all True for 66/66) | tests |
| project_reproduction | "not attempted" except PDE-040 | keeps published and reproduced claims apart |

### How the rows were found (search log) and what was left out

Not a systematic review. Searches were targeted, 2026-09-24, Europe PMC REST (`search`, `resultType=lite`, default
relevance order or `sort=CITED desc`), plus `paperclip` corpus search. Queries (abbreviated):

* POTS: `(POTS OR "postural orthostatic tachycardia") AND ("active stand" OR "stand test") AND tilt`; `TITLE:"postural
  tachycardia" AND (TITLE:"what is abnormal" OR TITLE:"normal")`; POTS AND (smartwatch OR wearable OR "Apple Watch"
  OR Fitbit OR photoplethysmograph*) AND (active stand OR orthostatic) AND (valid* OR accura* OR agree*); POTS AND
  (Holter OR 24-hour OR ambulatory) AND heart rate AND (diagnos* OR discriminat* OR ROC); POTS AND small fiber /
  sudomotor / neuropathy / skin biopsy (title); `AUTH:"Thieben MJ"`.
* ME/CFS: 2-day / two-day / repeated CPET AND (systematic review OR meta-analysis); 2-day CPET 2018-2026;
  CPET AND (AUC OR classification OR sensitivity); `AUTH:"Keller BA"`, `AUTH:"Snell CR"`, `AUTH:"Franklin JD"`,
  `AUTH:"van Campen"`, `AUTH:"Walitt B"`; cardiac autonomic regulation (title); actigraphy / accelerometer / physical
  activity (title); cerebral blood flow AND tilt (title); NASA lean test.
* Fibromyalgia: accelerometer / actigraphy / objectively measured (title); small fiber (title) AND meta-analysis;
  Sudoscan / electrochemical skin conductance; HRV AND meta-analysis; gait AND meta-analysis.
* Long COVID: CPET AND meta-analysis; HRV AND (systematic review OR meta-analysis); wearable / Fitbit / smartwatch
  (title); All of Us / RECOVER AND wearable; lean test; small fibre / corneal (title); pupil* / eye tracking /
  saccad* / oculomotor (title); voice / speech (title).
* Others: hypermobility AND orthostatic/POTS/autonomic (title); post-treatment Lyme; migraine AND (wearable OR
  pupillometry OR HRV OR eye tracking OR evoked potential) AND (diagnostic accuracy OR meta-analysis OR
  classification); mast cell activation AND (wearable OR device OR HRV OR tilt); wireless motility capsule /
  breath test / Gastric Alimetry; endometriosis imaging diagnostic accuracy / Cochrane (`AUTH:"Nisenblat V"`);
  bowel sound AND irritable bowel.

Inclusion rule (applied to every abstract read, whatever its direction): a device or objective physiological test,
a target condition, and a number comparing the condition with controls or a reference (or the share of cases with an
abnormal result, or how often healthy people cross the diagnostic threshold). Nulls were kept (7 null, 12 mixed rows).
The rule was written down during the searches, not before them; no row was dropped after reading because of its
direction.

Read but not tabulated (reason):
* Hedge 2026 test-retest of supine-to-stand tests in POTS (PMID 41887448), Uppal 2026 "To tilt or not?" (42235469),
  Robbins 2026 (42191429), Baker & Kimpinski 2019 normative HR (31476713), Oaklander 2024 (39159474), Juszczyk 2026 post-COVID POTS/IST (41932118): no abstract text available from Europe PMC and no
  open full text, so no number could be read.
* Grach 2025 voice in ME/CFS (40725538): online survey, not a device.
* Ruiz 2013 FM accelerometry (23794573): no comparison group. Zhang 2025 Fitbit in long COVID (41474983): no
  non-Long-COVID comparison. Cassilly 2008 SmartPill (18194154): healthy volunteers only. De Wandele 2014 hEDS
  (24968706): questionnaire only. Kaplan-type reviews of wearables in general were not added beyond PDE-052.
* The 2015 HRS POTS consensus statement (25980576) and the 2021 NIH POTS consensus (34144933): definitional; no open
  full text for the HRS statement; the definition row PDE-001 quotes a primary study's restatement instead.

Conditions with **no row**: MCAS, migraine, Lyme disease (acute), dysautonomia as a separate label (POTS rows cover
it). For migraine the devices found forecast attacks in already-diagnosed patients (not tabulated). No device study
with a diagnostic comparison was found for MCAS.

### Linkage strategy

None. Rows are aggregate published results about different people in different studies. A row never describes a
participant of NHANES, Stanford or MapMECFS. Rows link to the rest of the engine by concept only:
`condition_ids` -> `condition_registry`, `measurement_classes` -> `measurement_registry`.

### Limitations and caveats

* Targeted curation, not a systematic review: absence of a row is not evidence that no study exists.
* 35 of 66 rows are case-control designs (31 primary case-control studies plus 4 reviews that pool case-control
  studies), mostly against healthy volunteers (spectrum bias inflates accuracy).
* 17 rows are `diagnostic_accuracy`: 13 primary studies, none with validation in an external cohort (PDE-062 has a
  temporal hold-out of 15 + 15 from the same site), and 4 pooled reviews (external validation not applicable).
  Several choose thresholds or models in-sample.
* POTS and gastroparesis are **defined** by a device measurement (orthostatic HR rise; delayed gastric emptying), so a
  device's "accuracy" there is agreement with the defining measurement (incorporation bias), not independent validity.
* Endometriosis rows have an independent surgical reference standard; that is why their accuracy numbers mean more.
* Numbers were read from abstracts (58 rows), open-access full text (7) and one PDF (1). A verified quote shows the number
  was printed, not that the analysis behind it is sound.
* PDE-040 (Uwakwe 2025): the published HR-only (ROC-AUC 0.748) and HR + symptom (0.951) models could not be
  reproduced, because their HR window starts at a diagnosis date that the public release does not contain
  (`results/tables/stanford_reproduction_benchmark.csv`, row `uwakwe2025_rfhrm_rfcm`, `not_reproducible`). A
  different, pre-specified analysis by this project (whole-record resting-HR features) was null: AUROC 0.504
  (0.404-0.605), change over the demographic baseline -0.024 (-0.076 to 0.027) (`results/WEARABLE_STANFORD_RESULTS.md`).
  That null is not a failed replication of the paper's model. The symptoms-only model partly reproduced (0.862 vs
  published 0.907).

### Processed outputs

`data/processed/published_device_evidence.parquet` (66 rows; data_layer `measurement`; evidence_type
`published_biomarker`; object_id `published_evidence:<evidence_id>`).

### Reproduce

`uv run python -m measure_it.measurements.published_evidence` (fetches or re-reads the cached Europe PMC texts,
verifies every quote, writes the table and `registry_entry.yaml`); `--no-fetch` verifies against
`source_texts.jsonl` only.

73 rows have a `count_derivation` other than exactly "as stated in source_quote". Some are counts the curator computed (e.g. 409 − 66 controls), some are not reported or not applicable, and some say "as stated" and then add a note. The automatic count check runs only on the exact phrase. For the rows with an appended note, the curator checked the counts against the quote by hand.

### How the rows were found (search log)

Not a systematic review. On 2026-09-24, targeted searches were run against the Europe PMC REST `search` endpoint (`resultType=lite`, relevance order) through `measure_it.http`, and every candidate abstract was read before a row was written. The queries below are abbreviated; the full lists are in the curation hand-backs summarised here.

* **MCAS and look-alikes:**
  * urinary LTE4 + mast cell; PGF2 / prostaglandin + mast cell + urine; "baseline variability of serum tryptase"; MCAS suspected / real-life + tryptase; Valent MCAS consensus; Butterfield mediators; MCAS + tryptase + 11β-PGF2α; Buttgereit MCAS; 2012 "Definitions, criteria and global classification…";
  * KIT D816V peripheral blood mastocytosis;
  * Lyons "Elevated basal serum tryptase…"; HαT prevalence; HαT / TPSAB1 + EDS / hypermobility / POTS; "Genetically defined individual reference ranges for tryptase"; Shibao hyperadrenergic mast-cell activation;
  * mast cell + IBS meta-analysis / systematic review.
* **POTS:** POTS + norepinephrine; "600 pg"; hyperadrenergic POTS prevalence; POTS + blood / plasma volume / renin; POTS + autoantibody / G protein / autoimmune.
* **EDS/HSD:** 2017 EDS classification; hEDS + biomarker / gene / KLK15 / transcriptome / proteomics.
* **Long COVID:** exact titles for Klein 2023, Swank 2023/2024, Peluso 2024, Cervia-Hasler 2024, Pretorius 2021; RECOVER laboratory (Erlandson); microclots + flow cytometry, excluding the originating group; biomarker meta-analyses and systematic reviews; cortisol + Long COVID (no meta-analysis found); GPCR autoantibodies + Long COVID.
* **ME/CFS:** exact titles for Walitt 2024, Beentjes 2025, Esfandyarpour 2019, EpiSwitch, Theorell 2017, Blundell 2015, Montoya 2017, Corbitt 2019, Huth 2020, Germain 2022, Glass 2023, Loebel 2016, Naviaux 2016; NK cytotoxicity systematic review / meta-analysis; GPCR autoantibodies + ME/CFS nulls.
* **Fibromyalgia:** Oaklander small-fibre polyneuropathy; Üçeyler small-fibre pathology; passive transfer; satellite glia; FM IgG / autoantibody; cytokine meta-analyses; FM/a immunologic patterns; metabolic fingerprinting.
* **Lyme / PTLDS:** Leeflang and Waddell meta-analyses; modified two-tier; CCL19; PTLDS biomarker / antibody.
* **Migraine:** Tesfay 2022; CGRP meta-analysis / plasma level; Cernuda-Morollón 2013; Lee 2018.
* **IBS:** Pimentel 2015; vinculin / CdtB + IBS; calprotectin IBS meta-analysis.
* **Gastroparesis:** Grover 2011; gastroparesis + biomarker / autoantibody / autoimmune / serum.
* **Endometriosis:** Cochrane blood biomarkers; CA-125 meta-analysis; saliva microRNA and its validations; Moustafa 2020; Nisenblat 2019.

### Searched for but not included (and why)

* **Swank 2023 (CID, PMID 36052466) and Peluso 2024 (Lancet ID, PMID 38604216), spike antigen.** The abstracts carry no usable numbers or none is in Europe PMC, and full-text requests returned HTTP 500. The multi-cohort Swank 2024 (PLE-044) stands in for them.
* **Esfandyarpour 2019 (nanoneedle).** The full text returned HTTP 500, so its accuracy figure is not quoted and the row is qualitative (PLE-057).
* **Waddell 2016.** The pooled two-tier numbers are only in table images, so the row carries no numbers (PLE-092).
* **Menees 2015 calprotectin meta-analysis.** Not located by title. The newer Dajti 2023 meta-analysis is used instead (PLE-106).
* **Hirsch 2016 CA-125 meta-analysis.** Covered by the Cochrane review (PLE-111).
* **Not found:**
  * a meta-analysis of cortisol in Long COVID;
  * an independent blinded replication of fibrin-amyloid microclots;
  * an accuracy study of urinary mediators for MCAS with a reference standard that does not include the mediators;
  * a healthy-population false-positive rate for upright NE ≥600 pg/mL;
  * meta-analyses for MCAS mediators, POTS norepinephrine or POTS blood volume;
  * a meta-analysis of anti-CdtB / anti-vinculin.
* **Excluded as off-scope or better placed elsewhere:**
  * Okamoto 2024, a Valsalva haemodynamic marker for hyperadrenergic POTS: haemodynamic, so it belongs in the device table;
  * Kurin 2022, IBS–MCAS co-diagnosis in EHR (OR 16.3): not a lab;
  * Vernino 2024, IVIG RCT in POTS: a treatment trial;
  * Lyons 2025: a narrative review, covered by PLE-017/018;
  * Behm 2012 (FM/a precursor): no accuracy numbers;
  * Chen 2025 GPCR antibodies in 19,810 people: not a target cohort;
  * the Naviaux "revisited" letter and the Kell 2024 microclot rebuttal: no abstract.

### Known issues flagged in rows

* **PLE-026 (Trout 2026).** The abstract prints "161 (41%)" of 223, which is inconsistent (161/223 = 72%). The numbers are recorded as printed.
* **PLE-027 (Zhang 2014).** The cut-off is printed as "3.59 pg/ml", almost certainly a unit error. Recorded as printed.
* **PLE-058 (Hunter 2025).** The validation cohort size is reported inconsistently in the text (19/41 vs 24/45).
* **PLE-086 (Üçeyler 2011).** The IL-6 SMD sign is recorded as printed (the text says FM is higher).

### Linkage strategy

None. The rows are published aggregate results. They are joined to the rest of the project only through `condition_ids` (the condition registry) and `measurement_classes` (the measurement registry). Neither join is at the person level. The All of Us lab-concept counts that go with these biomarkers are in `docs/DATA_ACCESS_PLAN.md` ("Lab biomarkers in All of Us") and `results/tables/controlled_cohort_counts.csv`. They are counts of people tested in a separate controlled cohort, not people in any study cited here.

### Limitations

* The curation is targeted, so a missing row is not evidence of absence.
* Most comparisons are against healthy people (spectrum bias).
* MCAS mediators and hyperadrenergic POTS norepinephrine are part of the definitions they are judged against (incorporation bias).
* Almost no diagnostic-accuracy claim has independent external validation. Where one exists (salivary miRNA, PLE-116), accuracy fell sharply.
* Several high-accuracy claims come from test developers or sponsors (PLE-058, PLE-089, PLE-101/102, PLE-113–115).

### Files / endpoints retrieved
| file | url | bytes | sha256 | retrieved_at (UTC) | Last-Modified |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ReactomePathways.gmt.zip | https://download.reactome.org/97/ReactomePathways.gmt.zip | 298,479 | `8c1dbc8578431da5...` | 2026-09-24T04:34:10+00:00 | Wed, 24 Jun 2026 19:18:39 GMT |
| ReactomePathways.txt | https://download.reactome.org/97/ReactomePathways.txt | 1,592,393 | `f6d7a2bf89b5bcfe...` | 2026-09-24T04:34:11+00:00 | Wed, 24 Jun 2026 19:18:39 GMT |
| ReactomePathwaysRelation.txt | https://download.reactome.org/97/ReactomePathwaysRelation.txt | 634,259 | `fd49a624d80c14eb...` | 2026-09-24T04:34:11+00:00 | Wed, 24 Jun 2026 19:18:40 GMT |

The three release-97 archive files are byte-identical (sha256) to the `https://reactome.org/download/current/` files
that the first molecular-coherence build fetched on 2026-09-23 (current was release 97 then), so pinning the URL
changed no Test 3 number.

### Key variables
GMT: pathway name, stable id (R-HSA-...), member gene symbols (-> over-representation analysis). Pathway list:
stable id -> name, species (Homo sapiens kept). Relation file: parent -> child stable ids (-> top-level ancestors ->
physiological-system classification with the curated anchor table in docs/ANALYSIS_PLAN_MOLECULAR.md).

### Missingness
No missing identifiers in the three files (every row has a stable id; GMT rows have >= 1 gene).

### Linkage strategy
Gene symbols join to HGNC approved symbols (source `hgnc`); pathways join to Open Targets `reactome_top_level_term`
by stable id for a hierarchy cross-check. Condition-level only: nothing joins to a person.

### Limitations and caveats
* Pathway annotation, not measurement; enrichment of a public gene list is condition-level molecular context.
* Pathways are nested and overlap; pathway-level agreement p-values are anti-conservative (noted in Test 3).
* The live AnalysisService cross-check always runs the current release; its cached responses are what the tables use.

### Processed outputs
None in data/processed. Used by measure_it.omics.coherence (results/tables/test3_*.csv, Test 3 molecular coherence) and measure_it.omics.graph (measurable_biology, fig5_*.csv).

### Reproduce
`uv run python -m measure_it.omics.reference_data` (files are downloaded once and recorded in MANIFEST.json), then
`uv run python -m measure_it.omics.coherence`.
